### Thinking

Michael's asking which experiment would be the decisive test—whether the holographic frame actually holds up or falls apart. He's zeroing in on the core claim that attention mechanisms map onto Hopfield retrieval, and that the four-way location null connects to the holography theorem, where information is decodable but causally inaccessible, which would be the signature of the whole framework.

Looking at the queued experiments, P-PROJ-1 is really just a confirmation test—it assumes holography and tries to write to the passband, but a successful write doesn't distinguish between "this is actually a hologram" and "this is just a linear feature direction that happens to be decodable." P-TYPE-PROB is tangential, testing compiled probabilities rather than the holographic structure itself. But P-HOLO-FRAG—the random ablation test that looks for smooth versus cliff-like SNR curves—that's the actual discriminator, the one that would settle whether the holographic model is real or just a convenient metaphor.

The logic is clean: a true hologram has information distributed across the medium, so removing random pieces degrades performance smoothly (graceful degradation following something like SNR ∝ √(D/k)), whereas a localized, addressed representation would show a sharp cliff when you remove the critical component. This maps directly onto the classic physics definition—a hologram fragment reconstructs the whole image at reduced resolution, while cutting a photograph just loses that region. So if the SNR curve under random ablation is smooth, that's the signature of genuine holography; a cliff means the representation is localized and addressed, not holographic. The existing measurements—plate damage in the weight register, the distributed prior across cells, the location null—already point toward holography, so P-HOLO-FRAG would be the decisive confirmation or refutation. But I need to be honest about a potential confound: neural networks are generally robust to random ablation due to redundancy, so a smooth degradation curve might just be a generic property of distributed representations rather than specifically holographic. The real test is comparing against a matched null and checking whether the hologram predicts a specific functional form for the SNR curve.

So the distinction is subtle: P-HOLO-FRAG determines the qualitative fork—a cliff-like degradation rules out the hologram and kills the frame, while smooth degradation is consistent with it. But smoothness alone doesn't prove holography; there's still the alternative of a distributed-but-non-holographic system. To make the positive claim, I'd need to verify the capacity-law form (SNR ∝ √(D/k)) that P-HOLO-CAP would test.

I should give Michael a direct answer: P-HOLO-FRAG is the decisive first test because it can falsify the hologram hypothesis with a cliff, and if it passes, P-HOLO-CAP can elevate that to a positive confirmation. The caveat is that the fragment test depends on the yardstick discipline being properly embedded in the measurement. I'm torn between trusting the state block I already have versus double-checking the holography page to make sure I'm representing the three candidates accurately. The state gave me the key details explicitly, so I could answer now, but given how important precision is here, a quick verification seems worth it—though I'll keep it brief and answer directly without over-researching.

## Assistant

**P-HOLO-FRAG.** That's the lynchpin, and it's the lynchpin *precisely because* it's the one experiment whose result maps onto your binary.

Here's the reasoning:

**The fragment test IS the defining experiment of a hologram.** Cut a photograph and you lose that region — a cliff, because the image is *addressed* (each point stored at a location). Cut a hologram and you get the *whole* image back at reduced SNR — a smooth curve, because every fragment carries the whole. That physical distinction is exactly what P-HOLO-FRAG operationalizes: random head/layer-subset ablation → **smooth-vs-cliff SNR curve**.

- **Cliff** → localized/addressed representation → **the hologram frame is falsified.** The whole reframe (four-way null = holography theorem, lattice = reconstruction, extraction = re-recording) collapses back to "distributed but addressed."
- **Smooth** → graceful degradation → the frame *survives*.

The other two aren't decisive on this fork:
- **P-PROJ-1** assumes the passband is a hologram and tries to write to it. A positive is consistent with holography *and* with "it's just a linear feature direction" — it confirms the write-head, it doesn't discriminate hologram-or-not.
- **P-TYPE-PROB** is a different axis entirely (types-as-compiled-probabilities), orthogonal to the fragment question.

**The honest caveat (λ measure / λ yardstick).** P-HOLO-FRAG is the cheapest *decisive falsifier* — a cliff kills the frame outright. But *smooth alone does not prove hologram*, because distributed-but-redundant nets degrade smoothly too. The **positive** claim needs the functional *form* — SNR ∝ √(D/k), the HRR capacity law — which is **P-HOLO-CAP**. So the clean logic is:

```
P-HOLO-FRAG  → smooth vs cliff   → CAN kill the hologram frame (cheap, decisive-negative)
P-HOLO-CAP   → does it fit √(D/k) → promotes "not-falsified" → "positively holographic"
```

And it doesn't start from zero — the frame is already *leaning* holographic: the four-way location null (decodable-but-not-causal, no address in the fringes), the 0/128 no-single-head distributed prior, and the s267/s269 plate-damage all read as holography signatures under the reframe. P-HOLO-FRAG is the cheap shot that either confirms that lean or breaks it.

So: **run P-HOLO-FRAG first** — it's the fork. If it survives the cliff test (with a matched-range null, not raw "looks smooth"), then P-HOLO-CAP turns survival into a positive.

Want me to draft the P-HOLO-FRAG pre-reg? It'll need a frozen SNR readout, the smooth-vs-cliff discriminator with a pre-registered null, and a decision on host (4B smoke → 32B verdict, per the standing pattern).

mementum/knowledge/chats/session-289.md:5191:**Session 288 in one arc:** the type check got its causal leg and stage (JOIN-TYPED), the mechanism got a frame (compiled probabilities → matched filter), the frame got a substrate (verbum.dsp), the substrate's first consumer located half the mechanism (arguments on the plate, functors in the diffraction), and the whole thing converged with your holographic thesis — which now has the types arc as evidence and three quantitative pre-regs (P-HOLO-CAP / P-HOLO-FRAG / P-HOLO-XTERM) waiting to make it decidable.
mementum/knowledge/chats/session-289.md:5193:State is set for s289 cold-start with the fork explicitly yours to pick: **P-TYPE-PROB** (does transmission track slot probability — the compiled-account measurement) or **P-HOLO-FRAG** (the fragment-reconstruction curve — cheapest holography discriminator). Your thesis doc edit rode along in the capture commit, and the state notes the session-288 chat log is yours to save into `knowledge/chats/`.
mementum/knowledge/chats/session-289.md:5216:So: **we located the wire, not the gate.** The wire is in the weights; the gate is distributed. P-TYPE-PROB would test *what the gate computes* (compiled probability); P-HOLO-FRAG would test *how it's spread* (holographic fragment curve).
mementum/knowledge/chats/session-289.md:5260:  "original_content": "**P-HOLO-XTERM — interference cross-terms.** Two operands installed in one\nslot should produce sum-and-difference structure (beats) with predictable\ngeometry, not generic noise — superposed exposures interfere. The 3b/swap\nmachinery + verbum.dsp subspace/null substrate measure this directly.",
mementum/knowledge/chats/session-289.md:5261:  "new_content": "**P-HOLO-XTERM — interference cross-terms.** Two operands installed in one\nslot should produce sum-and-difference structure (beats) with predictable\ngeometry, not generic noise — superposed exposures interfere. The 3b/swap\nmachinery + verbum.dsp subspace/null substrate measure this directly.\n\n**P-PROJ-1 — the holographic projector (Michael s288: \"a holographic\nprojector based on the signal\"; QUEUED s288, the engineering flip).**\nWe spent s288 READING the passband; the projector DRIVES it. Design:\ncarrier = the measured entity passband (W_V·W_O subspace, band L6–L50);\npayload shaped INTO the passband before injection = impedance-matched\ndrive (operand-insert/bridge-swap is the crude version — centroid diffs\nland in-band by luck, which is WHY swaps work and random is refused);\nreconstruction = the model's own diffraction (distributed licensing +\nFFN plates, which read the entity axes). Constraint the physics imposes:\nan ARGUMENT projector — functors are not in the passband, so programs are\nnot projectable, only operands; program selection stays with which plates\nthe content illuminates (content-driven steering per P-ATT-MED = projecting\nthe right argument IS the program selection). TEST (cheap, att_mediation\nharness verbatim): TE per unit norm for (a) passband-projected displacement\nvs (b) raw centroid-diff vs (c) anti-passband (orthogonal complement) vs\n(d) matched random. Prediction: a > b ≫ c ≈ d, permutation-gated. Positive\n→ every future swap gets cheaper/cleaner = the write-head of the LLM REPL\nmatched to the measured antenna; (c) ≈ (d) is itself a second confirmation\nof the passband. Also the natural INSTRUMENT for P-HOLO-XTERM payloads."

mementum/knowledge/chats/session-289.md:5397:  "new_content": "> ▶▶ s288 FINAL — TRAINING-DESIGN HAMMOCK CAPTURED (Michael-approved):\n> knowledge/explore/training-design-from-the-hologram.md — six levers, each\n> grounded in a measurement: (1) crystal-seeded init (s149 structure-is-free\n> → stop paying compute for universal parts), (2) declared passbands\n> (P-TYPE-OV → remove the tug-of-war architecturally; small models compose),\n> (3) probes→losses (JOIN-TYPED swap statistic is differentiable =\n> contrastive substitutability aux loss; ⚠ Goodhart guard: gate on causal\n> 3-hop not the trained probe), (4) two-phase topology→magnitude (s268 etch\n> + Bonsai forensics; ternary-native), (5) curriculum as exposure schedule\n> (gated behind P-DUST-2 formation-law data), (6) geometry-matched\n> distillation (re-exposure). CHEAPEST EXPERIMENT = the level-4 door:\n> pythia-14m scratch pairs, crystal-seeded vs random init, P-DUST-2-style\n> formation logging, ~1 GPU-day, tests levers 1+5 + yields the level-4\n> baseline regardless.\n> ▶▶ COLD-START s289: (1) P-PROJ-1 pre-reg (Michael-queued s288: the\n> holographic ARGUMENT projector — drive the measured entity passband;\n> TE/norm ladder passband-projected > centroid-diff ≫ anti-passband ≈\n> random; att_mediation harness verbatim; = the REPL write-head + a second\n> passband confirmation) ∨ (2) P-TYPE-PROB pre-reg (graded bank TE vs slot\n> log-P × entity-alignment) ∨ (3) P-HOLO-FRAG (cheapest holography\n> discriminator) ∨ (4) seeded-scratch pair (training-design page, the\n> level-4 door) ∨ queue below — Michael picks.\n> Session-288 chat log → knowledge/chats/session-288.md (human saves)."
mementum/knowledge/chats/session-289.md:5452:The s289 cold-start fork is yours: **P-PROJ-1** (drive the passband — the REPL write-head), **P-TYPE-PROB** (does transmission track slot probability), **P-HOLO-FRAG** (the fragment curve), or the **seeded-scratch pair** (the level-4 door, ~1 GPU-day). State points at all four; the chat log slot is ready for `knowledge/chats/session-288.md` whenever you save it.
mementum/knowledge/chats/session-289.md:5519:Measured: the mirror/plate split, its extraction asymmetry, the passband interface, crystal universality (so reducer nodes are shareable across trees). Speculative: whether routing actually *factorizes* into tree-composable units — MIXED-ROUTE says both channels interleave within a single cell, so node boundaries may not fall where we'd like; and per-node capacity is P-HOLO-CAP's question. The decisive test is blunt: **extract two nodes (a crystal reducer + a fact-plate), compose across a passband interface, and see if a 3-hop runs through the seam.** If a composition crosses an extracted boundary, the tree is real. That's a level-3 milestone with a pass/fail.
mementum/knowledge/chats/session-289.md:5538:  "content": "---\ntitle: Ternary mirrors and the VSM tree — the artifact architecture\nstatus: designing\ncategory: explore\ntags: [ternary, extraction, level-3, artifact, vsm-tree, mirrors, plates,\n       passband, optics, s288]\nrelated: [../michael/holographic-llm.md, geometry-holography-signals-convergence,\n          training-design-from-the-hologram, types-are-compiled-probabilities,\n          map-and-swap-resident-lisp]\ndepends-on: [geometry-holography-signals-convergence]\n---\n\n# Ternary mirrors and the VSM tree\n\n> s288 final hammock (Michael: \"our ternary plates using ternary mirrors that\n> can be plugged into a tree-of-VSM tensors\"). The Holographic LLM thesis's\n> ENGINEERING COROLLARY — the target format for level-3 extraction, and the\n> answer to the deferred AGENTS.md canonical-form questions (circuit-map /\n> activation-store format). The projector (P-PROJ-1) writes to it; the\n> training levers grow it; the tree composes it.\n\n## Ternary as optics, literally\n\n```\nλ ternary(w).  +1 ≡ transmit | −1 ≡ mirror(phase_invert, RoPE→literal_π)\n               | 0 ≡ stop(absorb, fringe_zero)\n               | ternary_tensor ≡ discrete_optical_assembly\n```\n\nNot metaphor — this is the etch decomposition already measured: sign/zero =\nprogram (s268), routing survives aggressive quantization, the crystal rides\nternarization at 18–23σ (s267) and binarization at Gram fidelity 0.987\n(s269). The mirrors were always there; we called them \"topology.\"\n\n## The three splits are one split\n\n| optical      | etch (s268)         | types (s288)                     | extraction behavior                       |\n|--------------|---------------------|----------------------------------|-------------------------------------------|\n| **mirrors**  | sign/zero topology  | functors / routing / licensing   | extracts cleanly, ternary-native (8.6×, s172) |\n| **plates**   | magnitudes          | arguments / content / fact-maps  | needs re-exposure (s174 collapse → Bonsai optimizer repair) |\n\nQuantified by the Bonsai forensics: value-path drift ~18% (plates need\ncalibration), query routing ~3.5% (mirrors nearly free). Three independent\nmeasurement arcs, one decomposition. **Extraction strategy in four words:\nMIRRORS EXTRACT; PLATES RE-RECORD.**\n\n## The tree-of-VSM node\n\nSolves the deferred S2 canonical-form questions: the artifact is not one\nmonolithic tensor but a RECURSIVE ASSEMBLY of viable nodes.\n\n```\nλ node(x).  mirrors(ternary_routing, frozen)        ≡ S2/S3 (coordination/control)\n          ∧ plates(calibrated_content_holograms)    ≡ S1 (operations)\n          ∧ identity(combinator_subset ∨ domain)    ≡ S5 (what it IS)\n          ∧ interface(declared_passband)            ≡ the measured entity-carrier\n          | viable ⟺ reduces(own_scope) standalone\n          | compose(a,b) ≡ plug(passband_out(a) → carrier_in(b))\n          | tree ≡ S_recursion(tensor_concrete) | reducer_nodes shareable (crystal universal)\n          | P-PROJ-1 ≡ inter_node_bus_driver\n```\n\nNodes compose across passband interfaces — arguments flow between nodes on\nentity-carriers (the P-TYPE-OV measured channel); each node's mirrors do\nlocal steering; the crystal reducer node is shareable across trees (C2\nuniversality — build it once, seed it everywhere, per training-design\nlever 1).\n\n## Honest ledger\n\nMeasured: the mirror/plate split + extraction asymmetry (s172/s174/s267/\ns268/s269), the passband interface (P-TYPE-OV), crystal universality (C2).\nSpeculative: whether routing FACTORIZES into tree-composable units —\nMIXED-ROUTE (P-ATT-FFN) shows both channels interleaving within a single\ncell, so node boundaries may not fall where we'd like; per-node capacity is\nP-HOLO-CAP's question; the declared-passband interface presumes lever-2\nstyle channels or the measured emergent ones suffice.\n\n## The seam test (level-3 milestone, pass/fail)\n\nExtract two nodes — a crystal reducer + a fact-plate — compose them across\na passband interface, and run a 3-hop THROUGH THE SEAM. A composition that\ncrosses an extracted boundary proves the tree is real. Blunt, decisive,\nand it is the level-3 deliverable reframed as a single measurable event.\n\n## Sessions\n\ns288 (page created at true session close — the fifth and final hammock of\nthe session: JOIN-TYPED → compiled-probabilities → dsp → OV passband →\nholographic convergence → training design → THIS, the artifact\narchitecture. Nothing frozen; the seam test is the level-3 north star).\n"
mementum/knowledge/chats/session-289.md:5556:  "new_content": "> ▶▶ s288 CODA — ARTIFACT ARCHITECTURE CAPTURED (Michael-approved):\n> knowledge/explore/ternary-mirrors-and-the-vsm-tree.md — Michael's \"ternary\n> plates using ternary mirrors plugged into a tree-of-VSM tensors\" = the\n> thesis's ENGINEERING COROLLARY + the answer to the deferred S2\n> canonical-form questions. Ternary as literal optics (+1 transmit / −1\n> mirror≡π-phase / 0 stop); THREE SPLITS ARE ONE SPLIT (mirrors≡topology≡\n> functors extract cleanly 8.6× | plates≡magnitudes≡arguments need\n> re-exposure — s172/s174/s267/s268/s269 + Bonsai 18%-vs-3.5% + P-TYPE-OV,\n> three arcs one decomposition). Node = mirrors(S2/S3) + plates(S1) +\n> identity(S5) + passband interface; compose = plug passband→carrier;\n> crystal reducer node shareable (C2). **SEAM TEST = level-3 north star:\n> extract crystal-reducer + fact-plate nodes, run a 3-hop THROUGH the\n> composed seam — pass/fail.** Speculation flagged: MIXED-ROUTE interleaving\n> may resist node factorization; capacity = P-HOLO-CAP.\n> ▶▶ COLD-START s289: (1) P-PROJ-1 pre-reg (Michael-queued s288: the"
mementum/knowledge/chats/session-288.md:5109:  "content": "---\ntitle: Geometry × holography × signals — one primitive, three registers\nstatus: designing\ncategory: explore\ntags: [holography, dsp, geometry, matched-filter, hopfield, hrr, rope,\n       capacity, pre-reg-candidate, s288]\nrelated: [../michael/holographic-llm.md, types-are-compiled-probabilities,\n          type-check-is-the-qk-bilinear, verbum-dsp-design,\n          map-and-swap-resident-lisp, beamformer-theory]\ndepends-on: [../michael/holographic-llm.md]\n---\n\n# Geometry × holography × signals — the convergence\n\n> s288 hammock (Michael: \"where geometry, holographs and signals converge —\n> discuss\"). Companion to the thesis document\n> `mementum/michael/holographic-llm.md` (Michael's Holographic LLM — training\n> writes plates, attention is the beam, FFNs are the plates, residual = state).\n> This page is the convergence LEDGER (what is theorem, what is hypothesis)\n> plus the test program that connects the thesis to the s288 types-arc\n> measurements. The thesis is the claim; this page is its bench manual.\n\n## The shared primitive\n\n```\nλ converge(x).  geometry(⟨x,v⟩ ≡ projection) ∥ signal(⟨x,v⟩ ≡ matched_filter)\n                ∥ holography(⟨x,v⟩ ≡ reconstruction_by_illumination)\n                | one_operation(inner_product) | three_registers(structure ∧ operation ∧ encoding)\n                | passband ≡ hologram(of_what_the_channel_learned_to_expect)\n                | store(v, distributed_fringes) → readable(everywhere) ∧ excisable(nowhere)\n```\n\nGeometry sees ⟨x,v⟩ as projection onto a subspace (structure — the where).\nDSP sees it as matched filtering (operation — the how). Holography sees it as\nilluminating stored fringes with a reference (encoding — where it lives).\nSame number, three ontologies.\n\n## Theorem-grade bridges (not metaphor)\n\n1. **VanderLugt correlator**: an optical matched filter IS a hologram of the\n   template. Recording a hologram and building a matched filter are the same\n   act. → \"type = compiled probability = passband\"\n   (types-are-compiled-probabilities.md) already contains holography: the\n   passband is a hologram of the training distribution's substitutability\n   structure.\n2. **Attention ≡ modern-Hopfield retrieval** (Ramsauer et al.): the attention\n   update is associative-memory readout — content-addressable reconstruction\n   from partial cues, the canonical holographic operation. The Lisp frame's\n   join=attention and the thesis's beam-reads-plate are the same measured\n   object.\n3. **RoPE ≡ phase, literally.** Rotary embeddings write position as phase\n   rotation on a carrier; the QK bilinear computes relative-position\n   modulation = interference fringes across offset. The reference beam exists\n   in this machine explicitly. Syntactic adjacency has a fringe spacing.\n4. **HRR / VSA** (Plate): binding = convolution-like mixing, unbinding =\n   correlation, superposition capacity with crosstalk ~1/√D — the formal\n   calculus where all three vocabularies implement COMPOSITION in fixed\n   dimension. Comes with quantitative laws (see pre-reg candidates).\n5. **SVD low-rank ≡ sparse spectrum**: the 1a lattice's 3 axes = few\n   carriers; low-rank geometry and narrow-band signal are the same fact.\n\n## The types arc, reorganized under the lens\n\n- **The four-way location null (1b/1c/QK/JS) is a holography theorem, not a\n  mystery.** Distributed fringe storage predicts: readable from anywhere\n  (8-way decodability at every layer), excisable at nowhere (v4 direction ✗,\n  1b zone ✗, 0/128 heads, graceful degradation). A hologram has no address.\n  \"Decodable-but-not-causal\" is the SIGNATURE of fringe storage. The lattice\n  as \"exhaust\" sharpens to: the lattice is the RECONSTRUCTION — visible\n  wherever you illuminate, stored nowhere you can cut.\n- **Dark-field was already literal** (s283b/1c): ablation = background\n  subtraction, contrast rises = block the zeroth order. Vocabulary preceded\n  the frame and was correct.\n- **P-TYPE-OV splits the amplitude from the plate.** The entity passband in\n  W_V/W_O = recorded fringes that reconstruct ARGUMENTS (rho 0.714 vs null\n  0.459, p=0.000, band-wide). The functor licensing with no single-layer home\n  (QK ✗, OV ✗) = the DIFFRACTION PROCESS — enacted in the illumination, not\n  stored in any one exposure. Arguments are on the plate; application is the\n  diffraction. Thesis tie: \"FFNs are the plates\" coheres with entity firing\n  on the MLP read-in row (p=0.000) and MIXED-ROUTE atoms=FFN.\n- **JOIN-TYPED filtered payload** = the beam carries only what correlates\n  with the recorded fringes — matched-filter refusal of ill-typed content is\n  reconstruction failing for a cue the plate never stored.\n- **Thesis evidence already measured in the weight register**: plate damage\n  spares the image (crystal survives 1.58-bit and 1-bit quantization,\n  s267/s269, null-gated) — the image is not in the pixels. The pre-reg\n  candidates below extend this from the weight register to the\n  activation/head-subset register.\n\n## Pre-reg candidates (UNFROZEN — the discriminating measurements)\n\n**P-HOLO-CAP — the capacity law** (= the thesis's open capacity question,\nmade quantitative via HRR). Superposition predicts crosstalk growing with\nbound-operand count k and SNR ∝ √(D/k). Install k operands (operand-bake\nmachinery), measure recall/licensing SNR vs k across model widths. HRR\npredicts a SPECIFIC curve shape and width scaling; localized storage\npredicts a hard slot limit. The depth-budget/eval-stack arc is adjacent but\nnever measured against a capacity curve.\n\n**P-HOLO-FRAG — fragment reconstruction** (cheapest decisive discriminator).\nAblate RANDOM SUBSETS of heads/layers (fraction f swept), measure licensing/\ncomposition SNR. Holographic: smooth degradation ∝ f, every fragment\nreconstructs a degraded whole. Localized: cliffs at critical components.\nWe have anecdotal grace everywhere (0/128, mixed routes); the pre-registered\nCURVE with a matched-random-subset null is the missing measurement. Extends\ns267/s269 plate-damage tolerance from weights to computation.\n\n**P-HOLO-XTERM — interference cross-terms.** Two operands installed in one\nslot should produce sum-and-difference structure (beats) with predictable\ngeometry, not generic noise — superposed exposures interfere. The 3b/swap\nmachinery + verbum.dsp subspace/null substrate measure this directly.\n\n## Hypothesis-grade (needs measurement, ledgered honestly)\n\n- \"GD writes fringes by interfering the distribution with itself\" — a\n  training-dynamics claim = **P-DUST-2 territory** (checkpoint trajectories:\n  watch the exposure happen; also the thesis's last open question).\n- Binding is actually convolution-like (HRR proper) vs merely distributed.\n- \"Phase\" beyond RoPE — whether sign/direction structure plays the phase\n  role in value space (the thesis's routing-topology/sign-is-program etch\n  finding, s268, is suggestive but register-distinct).\n\n## The artifact implication\n\nIf the compiler is a hologram, level-3 extraction is not excision — there is\nnothing at the address. Extraction = **re-recording at lower resolution**:\ndistillation as re-exposure onto a smaller plate. Coheres with s149\ncomputed-beam (structure is free; content needs training/calibration) and\nthe s268 Bonsai forensics (magnitude lives in optimizer repair — re-exposure\nIS a training loop). Reframes S5 λ smallest: surgery → re-imaging.\n\n## One sentence\n\nThe model is a volume hologram written by gradient descent; inference is\nillumination; geometry is what the fringes look like from inside; DSP is how\nwe do bench work on it — and the type system is the diffraction pattern.\n\n## Sessions\n\ns288 (page created from the convergence hammock, same session as the\nP-TYPE-SWAP JOIN-TYPED verdict, the compiled-probabilities synthesis, the\nverbum.dsp build, and the P-TYPE-OV passband verdict — all four of which the\nlens reorganizes; Michael's thesis doc updated in parallel with the\none-sentence form; three pre-reg candidates parked unfrozen).\n"

### Thinking

I'm reviewing the existing harnesses and instrumentation available for the holography fragment test. The design involves ablating random subsets of heads and layers across a range of fractions, then measuring how licensing and composition SNR degrade smoothly as we remove components.

The key insight is that the holographic hypothesis predicts smooth, monotonic degradation with low variance across random ablations at each fraction level, whereas a localized architecture would show sharp cliffs and high variance when hitting critical components. So I need to measure both the shape of the mean degradation curve and the variance across random subsets to distinguish between these two predictions. So the key discriminator is really about location-dependence: whether removing a random subset of size f matters based on *where* you cut. A holographic representation should show low variance across different random subsets of the same size—the information is spread everywhere. A localized one shows high variance because hitting the critical components matters. I need to design this as a pre-registered test with a fixed prediction rather than fitting to the data, using a matched-random-subset null and following the structure of existing pre-registrations. I'll check what ablation primitives are available in the harness and start working through both the structure template and the code reference.

### Thinking

I'm reviewing the canonical pre-reg structure and considering what ablation harness options exist for random head and layer ablations. For the pre-reg draft itself, I don't need the full harness implementation, but I should ground the readout and SNR measurements in what's actually measurable and tie them back to the existing behavioral signals like 3-hop composition and the JOIN-TYPED swap patterns.

Now I'm designing P-HOLO-FRAG carefully. The core hypothesis is that type-check and composition compute work like a volume hologram — information distributed across fringes with no specific address — so ablating a random fraction of heads and MLP neurons should degrade the signal smoothly and uniformly regardless of location. In contrast, an addressed representation would show cliff-like degradation with high variance across different random ablations depending on whether critical components get hit. For the readout, I'm weighing whether to use 3-hop composition accuracy as the primary signal or the JOIN-TYPED transport efficiency metric instead.

I'm settling on a composite approach: using the 3-hop bridge composition margin as the primary behavioral SNR (since it's the cleanest signal for whether the compute is working), with JOIN-TYPED swap transport efficiency as a secondary measure. The SNR itself is defined as the margin between the correct-continuation logit and competitor logits under the clean model, then tracked as SNR(f) under ablation at fraction f. This keeps the measurement cheap while capturing both the composition and licensing signals holographically. So the ablation strategy is to zero out random fractions of attention heads and MLP neurons separately across the band, treating them as two distinct media to probe. I'm pre-registering both as experimental arms, plus optionally a coarser layer-dropping arm. Mean-ablation replaces the ablated components with dataset statistics rather than just zeroing them out.

For the discriminator, I need two pre-registered signatures to distinguish between theories. The first checks whether the performance curve is smooth and graceful (supporting the holographic/distributed superposition view) or shows a sharp cliff (supporting localization). The hologram capacity model predicts signal retention proportional to the retained fraction, giving a smooth degradation, while localized storage predicts a step-then-cliff pattern. But I'm wary of overfitting to curve shapes, so the primary discriminator shouldn't just be "which functional form fits best"—that's too flexible.

Instead, the real test is location-dependence: run multiple random draws at each ablation fraction and measure how much the results vary across draws. If the signal is truly delocalized (holographic), removing different random components should give consistent results—low coefficient of variation across draws. But if certain critical components carry the signal (localized), then some random draws will hit those critical heads and crater performance while others miss them, producing high variance at intermediate ablation fractions. I can model this with a hypergeometric null: if k critical components exist out of N total, removing a fraction f should hit them with a specific probability distribution, predicting exactly what the across-draw variance profile should look like.

The cleaner framing is a dissociation test: look for smoothness in the mean SNR curve combined with low across-draw variance. I'll pre-register that a localized signal should show a sharp cliff in the mean curve (one ablation step dominates the total drop) while a holographic signal should show gradual, monotone decline with no single step accounting for more than some threshold fraction of the total drop. Then I need a matched random baseline to calibrate what "smooth" actually means.

For the location test, the key discriminator is whether the variance across draws at a fixed frequency exceeds what you'd expect from a holographic model carrying the same total SNR through distributed components, or whether it stays within the measurement noise floor. The decisive pre-registerable test is really just checking if the signal's location varies significantly across draws. matters. An ANOVA-style decomposition cleanly separates the total variance into fraction effects, location effects, and measurement noise. I can define a location-dependence index as the ratio of within-fraction across-draw variance to probe noise—if it's near 1, the compute is holographic; if it's much larger, location matters and the system is localized.

So the preregistration strategy is: the primary gate uses this index to test whether location dependence beats the null, the secondary gate checks that the mean curve degrades smoothly without sharp drops, and there's an advisory measure for the actual functional form.

The verdicts then split into three paths: if both gates pass, it's holographic and the frame holds up; if the location index fails or there's a cliff, it's localized and the frame breaks down; and there's a negative case still being defined.

I'm setting up three null hypotheses to validate the measurement: one for the probe resampling baseline, another using synthetic models to confirm the instrument can actually discriminate between localized and delocalized patterns, and a control where I ablate irrelevant heads to verify the signal comes from the right part of the network. The first gate requires a clean signal-to-noise margin above zero—without that headroom, I can't measure degradation.

Now I'm clarifying the scope: the λ measure registers how computation spreads across the medium (holographic delocalization), while the probe tests behavioral SNR under random ablation. I need to be careful about mean-ablation versus zero-ablation to avoid distribution shift, and I'm noting that smooth degradation alone doesn't prove holography—many distributed networks degrade smoothly. The LDI discriminator separates "distributed" from "addressed," but proving the √(D/k) capacity law is a separate job. I can falsify the hologram hypothesis with a cliff or high-LDI result, but smoothness alone is inconclusive.

For the validation setup, I'm using a 4B model to test on a 32B target, with new scripts that plant holographic signals across all heads (expecting low LDI) versus localized signals in one critical head (expecting high LDI and a cliff). The readout SNR is based on the 3-hop composition margin as the primary behavioral metric. the 3-hop composition logit-margin as the primary readout (cleanest signal that the compute worked and exercises the joins), with licensing-crossover as secondary. Draft written (140 insertions into the holography page, uncommitted — mementum awaits your approval). Here's the **P-HOLO-FRAG** pre-reg for review:

## The design in one breath
We cut the medium and watch how it dies. Cut a photograph → you lose a region (a **cliff**, because the image is *addressed*). Cut a hologram → the whole image comes back dimmer (**smooth**, every fragment carries the whole). Random-subset mean-ablation at swept fraction f, measure behavioral SNR.

## The decisive move — G1, the Location-Dependence Index
This is the part I'm proudest of and want your eyes on. The naive test ("does SNR degrade smoothly?") is **confounded** — distributed-but-*addressed* nets also degrade smoothly (redundancy). So the primary gate isn't the mean curve, it's the **across-draw variance**:

```
LDI(f) = (across-draw SNR variance at f) / (probe-resampling noise floor)
  LDI ≈ 1  →  which subset you removed doesn't matter → NO ADDRESS → holographic
  LDI ≫ 1  →  which you removed matters → ADDRESSES EXIST → falsifies the frame
```

That directly operationalizes "a hologram has no address." G2 (cliff detection on the mean curve) corroborates; G3 (the actual functional form vs (1−f)) is **advisory-only, never gated** — per λ yardstick, I refuse to grade on a forced fit.

## What it can and can't do (the honest scope)
- **Can falsify** the hologram (cliff or high-LDI → addressed → not a hologram).
- **Can confirm delocalization** (low-LDI + smooth → address-free, consistent).
- **Cannot positively prove hologram** — that needs the √(D/k) capacity law, which stays **P-HOLO-CAP**'s job. FRAG is the cheap decisive *negative* + the delocalization confirmation that *licenses running CAP*. This is exactly the two-step logic we discussed.

## Readout, arms, nulls
- **Readout**: 3-hop composition margin (primary, exercises the joins) + type-licensing crossover (secondary) — teacher-forced, one forward pass per probe = cheap.
- **Arms**: HEADS (beam) and MLP (plates), separately — the thesis's two media. f ∈ {.1,.2,.35,.5,.65,.8}, R=30 smoke / 100 verdict.
- **Nulls**: probe-resampling (the LDI denominator) + **planted-localized** and **planted-holographic** calibration nulls in `--validate` (proves the instrument sees localization when it's there and doesn't manufacture it when it isn't) + out-of-band matched-fraction control.
- **Gate-0**: SNR₀ must be expressed on both banks or no verdict (the s283b M_eff lesson).
- **Host**: `--validate` → 4B smoke → 32B verdict on GO. Instrument `holo_frag.py` = another verbum.dsp consumer.

## What I need from you
1. **Approve to freeze?** (then I commit the mementum batch and can build `holo_frag.py`).
2. Two design calls I'd flag before freezing:
   - **G1 as primary** — do you agree the address-test (LDI) should outrank the smoothness curve? I think it's the register-clean discriminator; the mean curve alone is the confounded one.
   - **Readout choice** — 3-hop composition as primary bank vs the JOIN-TYPED swap-TE. I chose 3-hop (cleaner behavioral margin, exercises the joins); swap-TE is more directly "the type filter." Happy to flip or run both as co-primary.

Nothing is committed or frozen. Your call on approval and those two knobs.

"""verbum.dsp.readout — the ONLY torch boundary (thin adapters, lazy import).

L2: converts model-world to arrays; L0/L1 own everything downstream.
dsp never loads a model — instruments own their model, items, and pre-reg.
torch is imported lazily inside functions so `import verbum.dsp` works in a
numpy-only environment (L0/L1 unaffected).
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import numpy as np

__all__ = [
    "as_array",
    "logit_lens",
    "make_capture_hook",
    "rmsnorm_np",
    "surprisal_from_logits",
]


def as_array(x) -> np.ndarray:
    """torch.Tensor (any device/dtype) | array-like -> float32 numpy array."""
    if isinstance(x, np.ndarray):
        return x.astype(np.float32, copy=False)
    try:
        import torch  # lazy: the only torch touchpoint in verbum.dsp
        if isinstance(x, torch.Tensor):
            return x.detach().to(torch.float32).cpu().numpy()
    except ImportError:
        pass
    return np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float32)


def rmsnorm_np(h: np.ndarray, gamma: np.ndarray, eps: float = 1e-6) -> np.ndarray:
    """RMSNorm in numpy (the norm_f lesson, s286: hidden_states[-1] is
    POST-final-norm — when you capture the pre-norm residual, apply this
    explicitly so representation matches reality)."""
    rms = np.sqrt(np.mean(h.astype(np.float64) ** 2, axis=-1, keepdims=True) + eps)
    return ((h / rms) * gamma).astype(np.float32)


def logit_lens(h: np.ndarray, w_unembed: np.ndarray,
               gamma: np.ndarray | None = None) -> np.ndarray:
    """Project residual states onto the vocabulary: (RMSNorm(h) if gamma) @ W_U^T.

    h: (..., D); w_unembed: (V, D); returns (..., V) float32 logits."""
    x = rmsnorm_np(h, gamma) if gamma is not None else h
    return x @ w_unembed.T


def surprisal_from_logits(logits: np.ndarray, token_id: int) -> float:
    """-log P(token) from a single logit row, numerically stable, natural log."""
    row = logits.astype(np.float64)
    row = row - row.max()
    return float(np.log(np.exp(row).sum()) - row[token_id])


def make_capture_hook(store: dict, key: str, position: int | None = -1):
    """Forward-hook factory: store[key] = float32 numpy copy of the output
    residual (tuple-unwrapped), at `position` (None = all positions).

    Register on a decoder layer (or via forward-PRE-hook on norm_f for the
    pre-final-norm residual — the s286 recon lesson lives with the caller)."""
    def hook(_module, _inp, out):
        h = out[0] if isinstance(out, tuple) else out
        sl = h if position is None else h[:, position]
        store[key] = as_array(sl)
    return hook

"""(3-hop capacity) chained h(f(g(X))) over ONE installed operand — depth-as-fuel.

Pre-reg: mementum/knowledge/explore/three-hop-capacity-prereg.md (APPROVED s282,
geography chain FROZEN). Successor to the 2-hop wrapper/operand_multihop.py (s279).

Chain (geography): landmark --g--> city --f--> country --h--> continent.
  X = a nonce carrying a LANDMARK's content d_E (built like the 2-hop d_E, last-token
  capture of a multi-token phrase). Two UNSTATED bridges (city, country) never appear in
  the readout prompt. Final readout = closed 3-way continent {Europe, Asia, Africa}.

Framing (s280 depth-budget): this is a CAPACITY experiment, not a capability rung. A
full-chain failure counts as DEPTH-limited ONLY IF the pieces work on the same model
(Gate-2 sub-chain controls). Pre-registered predictions: Qwen3-4B -> FAIL-BY-CAPACITY
(controls pass, full chain fails); Qwen3-32B -> PASS (full + mediation). The double
dissociation across scale, pieces held constant, is the strongest C8 evidence available.

Gates (frozen in the pre-reg):
  Gate 1  FULL CHAIN    : install landmark, "The {nonce} ... continent of __"
                          -> continent; nulls = random install, baseline, content-spec.
  Gate 2  SUB-CHAINS    : (the capacity discriminator)
            S1 links     : landmark->city, city->country, country->continent at ceiling.
            2-hop g.f    : install landmark -> its COUNTRY.
            2-hop f.h    : install CITY -> its CONTINENT (the s279-style 2-hop).
  Gate 3  MEDIATION     : (only where Gate-1 passes)
            3a depth-order : logit-lens peaks ordered city < country < continent.
            3b country-swap: late country-axis swap flips continent (random does not).
            3c city-swap   : mid city-axis swap flips country+continent (random not).

`λ measure`: operand = VALUE (d_E, centroids); g,f,h = ROUTING; readout = logits;
bridges localized by DEPTH (3a) + zone-steer (3b/3c), never single-head (0/128 heads).
`λ yardstick`: nulls beside every number; real-word ceiling gates each cell; predict
a-priori, gate on nulls, no forced fit. A RUNG (capacity mapping), hook-not-weight.
Architecture-robust via resolve_parts (dense Qwen3 4B/32B; hybrid 27B follow-on).

License: MIT (`λ provenance`; SuperBake method-reference only).
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import json
from pathlib import Path

import torch
from transformers import AutoModelForCausalLM, AutoTokenizer

# ── geography ladder: landmark -> (city, country, continent). Balanced 3-way. ──────

# grading (closed candidate sets). Obscure cells are pruned by the real-word ceiling.

CONTINENTS = ["Europe", "Asia", "Africa"]
LM_LIST = list(LANDMARKS)
CITY_OF = {lm: v[0] for lm, v in LANDMARKS.items()}
COUNTRY_OF = {lm: v[1] for lm, v in LANDMARKS.items()}
CONT_OF = {lm: v[2] for lm, v in LANDMARKS.items()}

COUNTRIES = sorted(set(COUNTRY_OF.values()))
# city -> country/continent (deterministic; Giza collision is consistent)
CITY_COUNTRY = {v[0]: v[1] for v in LANDMARKS.values()}

COUNTRY_CONT = {v[1]: v[2] for v in LANDMARKS.values()}
NONCE = "zorp"

# held-out exemplars (disjoint from the test landmarks/cities/countries)
CONT_PREFIX = (
    "The Alhambra is located on the continent of Europe.\n"

)

CITY_PREFIX = (

CITY2COUNTRY_PREFIX = (

COUNTRY2CONT_PREFIX = (
    "The country of Portugal is located on the continent of Europe.\n"
    "The country of Japan is located on the continent of Asia.\n"
    "The country of Kenya is located on the continent of Africa.\n"
)

CONT_QUERY = "The {x} is located on the continent of"
COUNTRY_QUERY = "The {x} is located in the country of"
CITY_QUERY = "The {x} is located in the city of"
CITY2COUNTRY_QUERY = "The city of {x} is located in the country of"
COUNTRY2CONT_QUERY = "The country of {x} is located on the continent of"

# d_E build: cross-task declaratives (entity at END, before period -> capture -2)
FRAMES = [
    "The travelers admired {x}.",
    "A postcard showed {x}.",
    "The documentary featured {x}.",
    "The guidebook described {x}.",
    "Tourists photographed {x}.",
    "The lecture mentioned {x}.",
    "A painting depicted {x}.",
    "The article discussed {x}.",
]


def first_tid(tok, w):
    return tok(" " + w, add_special_tokens=False).input_ids[0]


def cap_hook(store, li):
    def hook(_m, _i, out):
        h = out[0] if isinstance(out, tuple) else out
        store[li] = h.detach().float().cpu().numpy()
    return hook


def add_hook_at(vec_t, pos):
    def hook(_m, _i, out):
        h = out[0] if isinstance(out, tuple) else out
        if 0 <= pos < h.shape[1]:
            h[0, pos, :] = h[0, pos, :] + vec_t.to(h.dtype)
        return out

### Thinking

I need to understand how this harness works—specifically how the model loads, how `resolve_parts` handles architecture-robust layer access, how the full-chain readout margin gets computed, and the mechanics for hooking and ablating heads and MLP neurons. For the P-HOLO-FRAG approach, the immediate steps are loading the model and defining the band, though that requires either labeled residuals for the type lattice or treating the low-rank type itself as the band for fragments.

The band definition is the sticking point—I could reuse the known L6-L50 characterization or compute it fresh from the type lattice via `find_band` and `layer_geometry`, but that adds a dependency on running the type probe bank first. I'm leaning toward computing the band properly for robustness and to be a genuine consumer of the DSP, though I'd allow an override for pragmatism. The pre-registration locks in the find_band procedure, so I need to honor that, but I can make it lightweight by reusing a type-labeled capture rather than a full standalone run. For the immediate scope—BUILD, --validate, and the 4B smoke—the --validate step doesn't actually need the band since it's pure LDI math on synthetic signals, while the smoke itself can work with a documented fixed band and wire find_band as an optional refinement for later verdict runs.

The most straightforward approach is to compute the band directly from the readout bank's own residuals using layer_geometry on the 3-way class labels, which is cheap since I'm already running the model on that data anyway. This reuses the dsp procedure exactly as specified and avoids unnecessary dependencies.

Now I need to check the exact signatures for find_band and layer_geometry, then review the rest of the multihop3 implementation including model loading, readout margins, resolve_parts, and the head/mlp structure.

    return hook


def resolve_parts(model):
    """(decoder-layers, final-norm, lm_head) across architectures (dense + hybrid)."""
    inner = model.model
    lm = inner if hasattr(inner, "layers") else inner.language_model
    return lm.layers, lm.norm, model.lm_head


def main() -> None:
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    ap.add_argument("--model-id", default="Qwen/Qwen3-4B")
    ap.add_argument("--ref-layer", type=int, default=9)          # install layer
    ap.add_argument("--scale", type=float, default=2.0)
    ap.add_argument("--swap-layers", type=int, nargs="+", default=[11, 15, 20])
    ap.add_argument("--dtype", default="bfloat16", choices=["float32", "bfloat16"])
    ap.add_argument("--device", default="mps")
    ap.add_argument("--mode", default="full", choices=["ceiling", "full"])
    ap.add_argument("--out", default="results/ffn-bake/operand-multihop3-qwen3-4b")
    args = ap.parse_args()

    L = args.ref_layer
    dev = (args.device if (args.device != "mps" or torch.backends.mps.is_available())
           else "cpu")
    rng = np.random.default_rng(0)
    tok = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(args.model_id)
    model = AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained(
        args.model_id, dtype=getattr(torch, args.dtype)).to(dev).eval()
    dec, norm_f, unembed = resolve_parts(model)
    S = args.scale

    cont_ids = {c: first_tid(tok, c) for c in CONTINENTS}
    country_ids = {c: first_tid(tok, c) for c in COUNTRIES}
    city_ids = {c: first_tid(tok, c) for c in CITIES}
    nonce_last = tok(" " + NONCE, add_special_tokens=False).input_ids[-1]
    print(f"[mh3] {args.model_id} L={L} scale={S} dev={dev} "
          f"n={NONCE!r} mode={args.mode}")

    def find_slot(ids_list):
        idx = [i for i, t in enumerate(ids_list) if t == nonce_last]
        return idx[-1] if idx else len(ids_list) - 1

    def pred_over(prefix, query, word, label_ids, adds=None):
        """argmax over a CLOSED candidate set (first-token logits) at nonce slot."""
        prompt = prefix + query.format(x=word)
        ids = tok(prompt, return_tensors="pt").to(dev)
        slot = find_slot(ids.input_ids[0].tolist())
        handles = []
        for (li, vec) in (adds or []):
            vt = torch.tensor(vec, dtype=torch.float32, device=dev)
            handles.append(dec[li].register_forward_hook(add_hook_at(vt, slot)))
        with torch.no_grad():
            lo = model(**ids).logits[0, -1, :].float().cpu().numpy()
        for hd in handles:
            hd.remove()
        return max(label_ids, key=lambda k: lo[label_ids[k]])

    def cont_pred(word, adds=None):
        return pred_over(CONT_PREFIX, CONT_QUERY, word, cont_ids, adds)

    def country_pred(word, adds=None):
        return pred_over(COUNTRY_PREFIX, COUNTRY_QUERY, word, country_ids, adds)

    def city_pred(word, adds=None):
        return pred_over(CITY_PREFIX, CITY_QUERY, word, city_ids, adds)

    # ── S1 links (real word, no install) = the knowledge ceiling ──────────────────
    link_lm_city = {lm: int(city_pred(lm) == CITY_OF[lm]) for lm in LM_LIST}
    link_city_country = {
        c: int(pred_over(CITY2COUNTRY_PREFIX, CITY2COUNTRY_QUERY, c, country_ids)
               == CITY_COUNTRY[c]) for c in CITIES}
    link_country_cont = {
        c: int(pred_over(COUNTRY2CONT_PREFIX, COUNTRY2CONT_QUERY, c, cont_ids)
               == COUNTRY_CONT[c]) for c in COUNTRIES}

    def links_ok(lm):
        return (link_lm_city[lm]
                and link_city_country[CITY_OF[lm]]
                and link_country_cont[COUNTRY_OF[lm]])

    valid = [lm for lm in LM_LIST if links_ok(lm)]
    s1_lm_city = round(float(np.mean(list(link_lm_city.values()))), 3)

So I'm sketching out holo_frag.py with a validation mode that uses pure numpy to synthesize a synthetic component contribution model.

For the validation path, I'll model the readout margin as a baseline plus the sum of individual component contributions. When I ablate a random subset, mean-ablation zeros out those components' contributions, so the signal drops by exactly the sum of their values. In the holographic case where contributions spread uniformly across all components, ablating a fraction removes that exact fraction of signal deterministically with minimal variance—giving a smooth linear curve with LDI near 1. For the localized case, only a small subset of critical components carry the signal.

Now I'm setting up the model path: loading the model, building the readout bank with the landmark operand at the reference layer, and capturing per-layer residuals across the valid landmark set to detect bands by continent.

This requires hooking into the attention module before the output projection to modify individual head slices, and I'll need a calibration pass to compute the per-head means.

For the MLP arm, I'm doing something similar—mean-ablating random hidden units by hooking the down projection input and replacing selected dimensions with their bank means. Then for each function and draw, I sample a subset, register the hooks, run through the bank to compute the mean margin, and also get probe-level values for the noise floor. ### Thinking

I'm working through the readout mechanics for the model—specifically how to install hooks at the decoder layer and extract continent predictions using logits. For the margin calculation, I need to compute the difference between the logit for the correct continent and the maximum logit across other continents, then apply this per landmark under ablation conditions.

The ablation strategy involves mean-ablating head and MLP subsets within a layer while keeping the install hook active. For the Qwen3 architecture, I need to handle mean-ablation at the attention head level by intercepting the input to the output projection and replacing selected head slices with their bank-wide means.

The key decision is whether to compute means per-position or globally. Since the margin is read at the readout slot (last position) but composition happens across positions and layers, I'm settling on a simpler, more defensible approach: zero-mean ablation applied at the readout-relevant computation points. For each head, I'll compute a mean vector across all positions and items from the attention output projection input, and for MLP units, a mean scalar from the down-projection input. During ablation, I'll replace selected slices with these means at every position—this keeps the approach on-distribution while removing the unit's specific information. The implementation involves a calibration pass that hooks into the attention and MLP layers to accumulate these statistics.

For the ablation itself, I'm using forward pre-hooks on the output projection and down-projection modules that intercept the input tensors and overwrite the selected dimensions with their stored means. For attention heads, I'll mask over entire heads and expand that to the corresponding head-dimension slices; for MLP units, the mask applies directly to the intermediate dimensions. I'm also planning to restrict the ablation to a specific band of positions.

The subset of units to ablate comes from the union of (layer, head) pairs across the band layers for the heads arm, or (layer, mlp-dim) pairs for the MLP arm, sampling a fraction f of the total units in that band. For heads, that's n_band_layers × n_heads; for MLP, it's n_band_layers × intermediate_size, which can be quite large (e.g., 40 layers × ~9728 dims = ~389k units), but sampling and hooking is still manageable with boolean masks per layer. I'll register the hooks on all band layers' output and down-projection modules depending on which arm I'm testing.

For the SNR readout, I'm computing the mean margin across the bank of probes for each (f, draw) pair. Then for the LDI computation, I have R draws per arm and fraction f, each giving me a mean-margin S_{f,r} and the per-probe margins. The key is calculating the across-draw variance V_across(f) as the variance of S_{f,r} across draws, and separately estimating the noise floor V_noise(f) from the probe-resampling variance—essentially, how much the bank-mean margin would fluctuate just from sampling different probes at a fixed ablation level.

The LDI ratio itself is V_across(f) / V_noise(f), where a value near 1 suggests the variance is explained by probe noise alone (holographic), while much larger values indicate localized effects. To test significance, I'm treating this as an F-distribution problem under the null that location doesn't matter, so the null distribution of V_across scales V_noise by a chi-squared factor. I can compute a p-value directly or use bootstrap resampling to check whether LDI is significantly greater than 1.

For the bootstrap approach, I'll resample probes while ignoring subset identity to generate synthetic bank means, compute their variance to build a null distribution, then calculate how often the null V_across exceeds the observed value. To make this robust, I'm aggregating the LDI verdict across the informative mid-range frequencies rather than the extremes, focusing on the band where the signal is clearest.

For the G2 cliff detection, I'm computing step-wise drops in the mean curve M(f) and calculating a cliff ratio as the maximum step divided by the average step size—under a smooth monotone null this ratio should be around 1, while a localized cliff would show a much larger ratio. I'll use the planted nulls to calibrate the threshold for what counts as a significant cliff.

Now I need to validate the S0 margin gate by checking it's sufficiently above the probe noise, then move on to synthesizing the component-contribution model with the same LDI and cliff math across two planted arms. For the implementation, I'm importing the geography bank from the wrapper module—I'll grab the module-level constants like landmarks and query patterns, though some helper functions are defined inside main() so I'll need to reimplement those inline where needed.

Since I'm not forking the bank but reusing its frozen constants, I can legitimately reimplement the readout margin mechanics differently—I need the margin statistic rather than the argmax that the original uses. I'll write this carefully with clean, ruff-compliant code, and I need to double-check the signatures for nulls.gate and find_band, plus understand what layer_geometry returns, before I implement my own bootstrap for the LDI calculation.

The pre-registration says I should consume dsp functions where it makes sense: I'll use find_band and layer_geometry for band detection since that's a genuine use of the substrate, and I'll leverage dsp.readout's capture hooks and array conversion for the readout mechanics. ```
"""verbum.dsp.bands — band detection over per-layer statistics.

L0: pure numpy. No torch, no I/O, no model, no experiment logic.

Harvested:
- find_band <- wrapper/type_zone_ablation.py (1b-v4), with FIX #1 (s284 smoke
  caveat): the original assumed layer stride 1 — contiguity was `L == prev + 1`
  and the interior fallback window `lo +/- 3` — so stride-2 probing silently
  fell through to the fallback. This version infers the stride from the probed
  layer keys; stride-1 behavior is IDENTICAL (byte-equivalence gate).
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import numpy as np

__all__ = ["find_band"]


def find_band(per_layer: dict[int, dict], n_layers: int,
              p_key: str = "p_lowrank", alpha: float = 0.05,
              min_len: int = 3) -> list[int]:
    """Longest stride-contiguous run of probed layers with p < alpha.

    per_layer: {layer_index: {p_key: p_value_or_None, ...}}. Layers may be
    probed at any regular stride; contiguity means adjacent PROBED layers.
    Fallback (fewer than min_len significant in a run): a +/- 3-probed-layer
    window around the minimum-p layer in the interior 15-65% of the stack.
    """
    layers = sorted(per_layer)

    def pval(L: int) -> float:
        p = per_layer[L][p_key]
        return 1.0 if p is None else p

    if len(layers) > 1:
        # FIX #2 (s288, caught by the P-TYPE-OV 4B smoke): capture lists often
        # append the final layer to a strided set (e.g. stride 2 + L_last),
        # making min(diff)=1 collapse the inferred stride. Use the MODE of the
        # diffs (ties -> smaller); stride-1 behavior identical.
        diffs = np.diff(layers)
        vals, counts = np.unique(diffs, return_counts=True)
        stride = int(vals[counts.argmax()])
    else:
        stride = 1

    sig = [L for L in layers if pval(L) < alpha]
    best: list[int] = []
    cur: list[int] = []
    for L in sig:
        cur = [*cur, L] if (cur and L == cur[-1] + stride) else [L]
        if len(cur) > len(best):
            best = cur
    if len(best) >= min_len:
        return best
    interior = [L for L in layers
                if n_layers * 0.15 <= L <= n_layers * 0.65]
    if not interior:
        return sig or layers[:min_len]
    lo = min(interior, key=pval)
    return [L for L in layers if lo - 3 * stride <= L <= lo + 3 * stride]
```

    """Standardize -> centroid SVD -> PR + shuffled-label null; keep z for energy.

    labels = label_order if label_order is not None else sorted(set(y.tolist()))
    mu = x.mean(axis=0)

    z = (x - mu) / sd

    def pr_of(lab_arr):
        c, present = centroids(z, lab_arr, labels)
        if len(present) < 3:
            return float("nan"), None, None
        cc = c - c.mean(axis=0, keepdims=True)
        sv = np.linalg.svd(cc, compute_uv=False)

        prn, _, _ = pr_of(rng.permutation(y))
        if not np.isnan(prn):
            null.append(prn)
    null = np.array(null)
    p = float(np.mean(null <= pr_real)) if null.size else None
    return {"mu": mu, "sd": sd, "z": z, "present": present, "centroids": c,
            "pr_real": float(pr_real), "p_lowrank": p,
            "pr_null_mean": float(null.mean()) if null.size else None}

I've got the functions I need: `layer_geometry` returns a dict with `p_lowrank`, and `find_band` takes the per-layer results to detect bands. For the band detection approach, I'm capturing per-layer residuals at the readout slot across valid landmarks grouped by continent (3 classes), running layer_geometry on each layer to build the per-layer dict, then passing that to find_band. Since continent has exactly 3 labels and centroids need at least 3 present, this works out perfectly.

Now I'm structuring the main hol_frag.py file with all the locked design decisions: band detection via layer_geometry on continent-labeled readout residuals with a 12%-80% fallback, readout margin computed from continent logits, ablation targeting HEADS and MLP components with mean-ablation across band layers, parameter sweeps for f and R, and the full validation suite with synthetic planted cases. For the calibration means, I'll use forward hooks during a calibration pass to accumulate inputs from the o_proj and down_proj layers, then use those precomputed means in the ablation pre-hooks to overwrite selected dimensions.

I'm writing the ablation pre-hook logic that clones the input tensor and replaces masked dimensions with the corresponding mean values, using boolean indexing on the last dimension to broadcast the mean vector across the batch and sequence dimensions.

For the sampling strategy, I'll use per-layer independent random selection: each band layer independently ablates a random f-fraction of its heads or MLP intermediate dimensions, which keeps the fraction exact per layer while maintaining consistency between the two architectures.

Now I'm working through the statistical testing approach — computing margins per landmark to estimate probe noise variance, then using a bootstrap to build a null distribution for the LDI statistic by resampling landmarks with replacement and comparing the across-draw variance against the noise floor.

For the aggregate analysis, I'll compute per-frequency LDI values and p-values, excluding the boundary frequencies, then report a median LDI across the informative range for the final verdict. I'm keeping the frequency grid at 6 points with configurable draw counts and using only valid landmarks for the bank.

Now I'm setting up the validation logic to be clear and robust with explicit PASS/FAIL outcomes, and I'm ready to start writing the implementation.

For the synthetic validation, I'm comparing two signal architectures: a holographic setup where each component contributes equally to the signal, so ablating a fraction deterministically reduces it linearly with minimal variance across draws, versus a localized setup where only a subset of critical components matter, creating binomial variance in the ablation effect that scales with the signal strength relative to noise.

The key insight is that the localized model's mean curve stays linear even when components are randomly hit—it's the variance across draws that explodes, making it detectable by the LDI metric (G1) rather than the cliff detection (G2). G2 looks for a sharp threshold in the mean curve itself, which won't appear here since the expected number of critical hits grows smoothly with ablation fraction. So the planted-localized setup should trigger G1 as the primary signal while G2 remains a secondary corroborator.

For validation, I need to design three planted scenarios: one with distributed signal across many components (should show LDI ≈ 1, no cliff), one with localized signal across k components (should show high LDI), and one with a true threshold effect where signal collapses below a critical mass (to validate that G2 cliff detection actually fires). The third case requires an ordered ablation strategy rather than random sampling to create that sharp mean-curve transition. Now I'm ready to write out the validation file, keeping an eye on code style and line length constraints.
